[10] reconstruct current pressures at unobserved junctions from sparse same-time pressures. Hydraulic simulations vary operating parameters; random sensor masks expose the model to changing observation layouts. Masked inputs are zero-filled and loss is computed at masked positions. For L-Town, 10,000 generated snapshots are split 60/20/20. A separate one-week demand-pattern series sampled every five minutes provides the temporal-pattern test (Sec.6.4). Sequential testing still processes each snapshot independently: there is no future-pressure target.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"<b>Model and EDA.</b> Projected pressure features pass through graph-attention blocks with residual connections and mean aggregation, then a linear pressure output. [17] provide the closest physical-input overlap with our proposal. EILF (estimation-identification-localization framework) is a statistical/hydraulic diagnostic framework, not a trained future-pressure regressor. It is reviewed separately from the five learning models so that data decomposition is not mislabeled as a supervised forecast.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"<b>Task, preparation and model.</b> The journal study uses labeled 2018 data for development and 2019 for testing. It treats Area C separately and combines Areas A/B because B has no separate inlet-flow meter. In C, pump inflow minus change in tank storage estimates sector inflow; subtracting aggregate AMR demand yields a diagnostic residual. The authors observe level-quantization inconsistencies and compute C inflow/demand over 30-minute intervals. For A/B, empirical mode decomposition (EMD) extracts a seasonal consumption component from C, combined with weekly demand patterns to estimate normal flow.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Additive seasonal-trend decomposition using LOESS (STL) separates pressure trend, periodic behavior and residuals. Multiscale differences of flow residuals support three-sigma statistical process control and sliding-window incipient-leak assessment. Weekly pressure differences then enter a differential-search hydraulic localization procedure. The 2020 conference proposal [13] mentions VAR, whereas the journal formulation [17] uses STL; these versions must not be treated as one fully specified future-pressure model.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"<b>Performance and availability.</b> The journal conclusion (p.11) reports 16 detected/localized leakage cases in 2019, TPR=47.83%, FP=5 and an economic score of EUR167,981. All results below are the authors\' published outcomes; source/code inspection does not constitute reproduction of training.')]

```text
cheb_sam=[
h('3.2 ChebNet: forecasting and reconstruction as complementary diagnostic models'),
p('<b>Task and preparation.</b> Gardarsson, Boem and Toni [3] train a predictor of next-step nodal pressure and a reconstructor of the current pressure field. Both learn from pressure generated by the nominal hydraulic model; sparse pressure observations and topology are inputs, while full simulated nodal pressures supply labels. Their difference is used to diagnose leaks in official SCADA, including the 2019 evaluation series. Graph-weight/scaling choices, observed-node handling and historical windows are explored; residual plots examine how leakage changes the learned relationships.'),
p('<b>Model and time alignment.</b> Four Chebyshev graph-convolution layers propagate pressure features through the pipe graph. Hidden polynomial orders [240,120,20] and filter sizes [120,60,30], SiLU hidden activations, sigmoid output and Adam/weight decay define the inspected architecture [3,6]. History is stacked as input channels. T=3 is selected from {1,2,3,6,12,24}; at five-minute sampling it is a 15-minute history. The pinned loader ends X at row i-1 and labels row i: the forecast lead is +5 minutes, not +15.'),
p('<b>Performance and significance.</b> Predictor-minus-reconstructor residuals are mapped from nodes to edges and analyzed with seven-day moving statistics, alpha=1 and a one-alarm-per-day/six-hop neighborhood filter. The article reports 19 TP, 138 FP, 4 FN, TPR=82.61% and EUR326,521 for leakage diagnosis (Table 1, pp.5-6). A match requires an alarm during the leak lifespan and within 300 m. This demonstrates useful sparse graph inference, but also a substantial false-alarm burden; it is not a reported pressure MAE for n1(+15).'),
p('<b>Code and transfer to our study.</b> '+link(cheb+'utils/data_loader.py#L285-L290','Window/label construction')+' and '+link(cheb+'predict.py#L120-L131','preparation and chronological 80/20 split')+' are directly inspectable [6]. Scaling precedes that split in the selected script; our protocol fits transforms on training only. A nominal healthy reference for diagnosis and a forecast intended to follow recorded pressure during a leak have different target meanings.'),
h('3.3 SAM-kNN: adaptive local virtual pressure sensors'),
p('<b>Task, data and preparation.</b> Jakob et al. [4] simulate ten three-month WNTR scenarios on modified L-Town Area A (661 nodes, 766 links, 29 pressure sensors), sampled every five minutes. Five scenarios have one leak each; five have a sensor fault. These are newly generated scenarios, not the original 2019 SCADA mixture. The selected '+link(sam+'LeakDbScenarioPreprocessing.py#L19-L27','preprocessor')+' averages three preceding rows and labels the fourth. For each target sensor, its own input channel is excluded: the virtual sensor forecasts the next reading from the other sensors.'),
p('<b>Model and evaluation.</b> Short-term, long-term and combined memories generate competing kNN predictions; tracked errors select a memory, with cleaning/compression to retain useful older concepts while adapting. The pinned experiment skips the first 100 raw samples, initializes from 3,000 processed rows and then predicts batches of 200 before updating. Pump/PRV connections receive special adjacency weights (300 and 1, respectively), while other edges use a hydraulic-proximity weighting derived from pipe properties (Secs.2.1,3.1).'),
p('<b>Learning model.</b> AGN starts from a gated graph/GRU formulation. Weighted neighbor propagation exchanges local information; self-attention connects distant node histories; external attention combines pressure states with elevation/use attributes; gated hidden states and a cascade output layer generate the future sequence. Training loss uses sensor-node targets; non-sensor outputs are generalized using nodal attributes. The authors compare AGN with GGNN and graph-head reconstruction followed by GRU (GHR+GRU). # Milestone 2 · objetivo y aporte candidato

**Estado: propuesta en discusión.** No hay entrenamiento, comparación de modelos propios ni novedad científica confirmada.

## Objetivo que conservamos

Pronosticar la presión registrada en **n1 del Área C** a +15 minutos, con historia n1/n4/n31, nivel T1, caudal PUMP_1, AMR y calendario disponibles al emitir el pronóstico.

Ejemplo: a las 10:00, utilizar información conocida hasta las 10:00 para emitir el pronóstico de la lectura de las 10:15. Los registros de las 10:05, 10:10 y 10:15 no pueden entrar como predictores.

El dataset registra cada cinco minutos y la presión corresponde a un promedio del intervalo precedente. La lectura etiquetada 10:15 representa ese registro agregado; no es una presión instantánea inventada. Tres pasos es el horizonte elegido, no un retardo hidráulico identificado.

## Qué ya tiene antecedentes

- Garðarsson y AIGNN combinan reconstrucción actual y predicción del siguiente paso para diagnóstico.
- SAM-kNN ya realiza predicción temporal y adaptación en escenarios propios.
- LILA estima referencias entre presiones y caudales para producir residuos de fugas.
- Topología, sensores escasos, interpretabilidad, ruido y recalibración aparecen en otros trabajos de L-Town.

Por ello, cambiar +5 a +15 minutos, escoger Random Forest, modelar incrementos o usar topología no demuestra novedad por sí solo. Evitar información futura es un requisito de validez.

## Pregunta de extensión recomendada

¿Qué información conviene utilizar para pronosticar a +15 minutos cuando algunas mediciones hidráulicamente relacionadas están más atrasadas que otras?

Se propone desarrollar una **regla de selección o uso de mediciones que considere relación hidráulica y antigüedad de la lectura**, bajo un presupuesto explícito de sensores. La regla todavía debe definirse matemáticamente y confrontarse con literatura específica; no se presenta como un algoritmo terminado.

Por ejemplo, al emitir el pronóstico a las 10:00, un sensor relacionado podría tener última lectura a las 09:50 y otro menos relacionado, a las 10:00. Comparar ambas fuentes permite investigar el valor de la información y su frescura.

## Comparaciones necesarias
```

```text
def tex(s):
 return ''.join({'\\':r'\textbackslash{}','&':r'\&','%':r'\%','$':r'\$','#':r'\#','_':r'\_','{':r'\{','}':r'\}','~':r'\textasciitilde{}','^':r'\textasciicircum{}'}.get(c,c) for c in s)
class HTMLTex(HTMLParser):
 def __init__(self):super().__init__();self.parts=[]
 def handle_starttag(self,tag,attrs):
  attr=dict(attrs)
  if tag=='b':self.parts.append(r'\textbf{')
  elif tag=='font':self.parts.append(r'\texttt{')
  elif tag=='link':self.parts.append(r'\href{'+attr['href'].replace('%',r'\%').replace('#',r'\#')+'}{')
  elif tag=='br':self.parts.append(r'\par ')
 def handle_endtag(self,tag):
  if tag in ['b','font','link']:self.parts.append('}')
 def handle_data(self,s):self.parts.append(tex(s))
def html(s):p=HTMLTex();p.feed(s);return ''.join(p.parts)
def table(rows,widths):
```

### aggregated_output

````text
---
name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter
from pypdf.generic import NameObject

reader = PdfReader(input_pdf)
writer = PdfWriter()
writer.clone_document_from_reader(reader)

# Restores widgets that are missing from /AcroForm/Fields.
writer.reattach_fields()
fields = writer.get_fields() or {}
missing = set(expected_values) - set(fields)
if missing:
    raise ValueError(f"Form fields not found after repair: {sorted(missing)}")

values_to_write = dict(expected_values)
if flatten:
    # Paint every existing value before removing every widget.
    values_to_write = {
        name: field.get("/V", "/Off" if field.get("/FT") == "/Btn" else "")
        for name, field in fields.items()
    }
    values_to_write.update(expected_values)

writer.update_page_form_field_values(
    None, values_to_write, auto_regenerate=False, flatten=flatten
)

if flatten:
    # pypdf's flatten=True paints appearances but does not remove widgets.
    writer.remove_annotations(subtypes="/Widget")
    writer.root_object.pop(NameObject("/AcroForm"), None)

with open(output_pdf, "wb") as stream:
    writer.write(stream)
```

4. Reopen the written PDF before delivery. For an interactive result, require every expected field to be present in `get_fields()` with the expected `/V`, enumerate page widgets again, and confirm their effective `/V` (the widget value or inherited `/Parent` value) agrees. Confirm each updated widget has a non-empty `/AP` `/N` appearance and render the final pages to catch stale or clipped appearances. Do not rely on `/NeedAppearances` or a successful PNG render as proof that logical field data was updated.
5. For a flattened result, require zero `/Widget` annotations and no remaining `/AcroForm` field tree after reopening, then render the final pages. Keep an editable copy when the user may need to revise the form.

## Temp And Output Conventions

- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies

Prefer the Codex bundled workspace/runtime dependencies when available. The primary runtime is expected to include:

- Python packages: `reportlab`, `pdfplumber`, `pypdf`
- Rendering tools: `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo` from Poppler

If a dependency is missing, install only what is needed.

Python packages:

```bash
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

If `uv` is unavailable:

```bash
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

System tools for rendering:

```bash
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment

No required environment variables.

## Rendering Command

```bash
pdftoppm -png "$INPUT_PDF" "$OUTPUT_PREFIX"
```

## Quality Expectations

- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final Checks

- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers, footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

## Final response

### Final Response Citations

- **Create/edit:** for created or edited PDFs, cite each final deliverable exactly once with `purpose="output"`, in its own paragraph. Briefly describe the result without repeating its filename, path, or link. Never put output directives inside prose or list items.
- **Q&A and summaries:** Every file whose contents you summarize or use to support an answer MUST receive a `:codex-file-citation{...}` with `purpose="source"` in the final response. Use the source citation in place of the filename, wherever the filename would appear. Do not write the filename separately and append its citation at the end. Files merely inspected but not used in the answer need no citation. Do not emit output citations for unchanged files or read-only answers.

- Emit `:codex-file-citation{...}` directly, without backticks, code fences, or a Markdown-link wrapper. Use verified targets only. No need to cite or reference intermediate work.

For file citations, set `path` to the verified absolute file path on the task host. Omit `mode`.

Some illustrative examples, real responses use verified targets outside code fences.

```text
:codex-file-citation{path="/absolute/path/review.pdf" purpose="output"}
```

```text
The review of :codex-file-citation{path="/absolute/path/review.pdf" purpose="source"} is due Friday.
```


### Additional Citation Guidance

For PDF Q&A, inspect complete relevant pages and preserve material headings, table/figure labels, footnotes, sources, and sample sizes. PDF citations support only plain file citations; do not add `artifact_kind`, `page_number`, or other locators.

- La referencia es `Milestone 1/Milestone1.tex` y su PDF evaluado.
- Consultar `Plantilla/ESTANDAR_VISUAL.md` y usar `Plantilla/milestone_visual.tex` para los informes posteriores.

- Conservar originales y versiones anteriores. Un borrador no se marca como entregado o aprobado sin evidencia.
- Antes de publicar/entregar, compilar y revisar visualmente todas las páginas; comprobar tablas, desbordamientos, referencias y longitud requerida.

The storage block S has four features: L(v)-L(v-w) and the following estimated outlet-flow proxy for each w. The pump mean uses the w/5 right-end readings in (v-w,v], in m3/h:'),\nmath(r'\\widetilde Q_C(v;w)=\\overline Q_{\\mathrm{PUMP\\_1},(v-w,v]}-A\\frac{L(v)-L(v-w)}{w/60},\\qquad A\\simeq201.06\\,\\mathrm{m}^2.'),\np('For each window choose the freshest endpoint v<=u for which both level endpoints and every required pump reading have arrived. is a meta estimator that fits a number of decision tree regressors on various sub-samples of the dataset and uses averaging to improve the predictive accuracy and control over-fitting. Trees in the forest use the best split strategy, i.e. equivalent to passing `splitter=\"best\"` to the underlying cite629†`DecisionTreeRegressor` . The sub-sample size is controlled with the `max_samples` parameter if `bootstrap=True` (default), otherwise the whole dataset is used to build each tree.\nL638: This estimator has native support for missing values (NaNs). During training, the tree grower learns at each split point whether samples with missing values should go to the left or right child, based on the potential gain. When predicting, samples with missing values are assigned to the left or right child consequently. If no missing values were encountered for a given feature during training, then samples with missing values are mapped to whichever child has the most samples.\nL639: For a comparison between tree-based ensemble models see the example cite630†Comparing Random Forests and Histogram Gradient Boosting models .\nL640: \nL641: Read more in the cite631†User Guide .\nL642: \nL643: Parameters:\nL644: \nL645: \nL646: n_estimators int, default=100\nL647: \nL648: \nL649: The number of trees in the forest.\nL650: \nL651: Changed in version 0.22: The default value of `n_estimators` changed from 10 to 100 in 0.22.\nL652: \nL653: criterion{“squared_error”, “absolute_error”, “poisson”}, default=”squared_error”\nL654: The function to measure the quality of a split. Supported criteria are “squared_error” for the mean squared error, which is equal to variance reduction as feature selection criterion and minimizes the L2 loss using the mean of each terminal node, “absolute_error” for the mean absolute error, which minimizes the L1 loss using the median of each terminal node, and “poisson” which uses reduction in Poisson deviance to find splits, also using the mean of each terminal node.\nL655: Added in version 0.18: Mean Absolute Error (MAE) criterion.\nL656: \nL657: Added in version 1.0: Poisson criterion.\nL658: \nL659: Changed in version 1.9: Criterion `\"friedman_mse\"` was deprecated.\nL660: \nL661: max_depth int, default=None\nL662: \nL663: \nL664: The maximum depth of the tree. If None, then nodes are expanded until all leaves are pure or until all leaves contain less than min_samples_split samples.\nL665: \nL666: min_samples_split int or float, default=2\nL667: \nL668: \nL669: The minimum number of samples required to split an internal node:\nL670:   * If int, then consider `min_samples_split` as the minimum number.\nL671: \nL672:   * If float, then `min_samples_split` is a fraction and `ceil(min_samples_split * n_samples)` are the minimum number of samples for each split.\nL673: \nL674: Changed in version 0.18: Added float values for fractions.\nL675: \nL676: min_samples_leaf int or float, default=1\nL677: The minimum number of samples required to be at a leaf node. A split point at any depth will only be considered if it leaves at least `min_samples_leaf` training samples in each of the left and right branches. This may have the effect of smoothing the model, especially in regression.\nL678: \nL679:   * If int, then consider `min_samples_leaf` as the minimum number.\nL680: \nL681:   * If float, then `min_samples_leaf` is a fraction and `ceil(min_samples_leaf * n_samples)` are the minimum number of samples for each node.\nL682: Changed in version 0.18: Added float values for fractions.\nL683: \nL684: min_weight_fraction_leaf float, default=0.0\nL685: \nL686: \nL687: The minimum weighted fraction of the sum total of weights (of all the input samples) required to be at a leaf node. Samples have equal weight when sample_weight is not provided.\nL688: \nL689: max_features{“sqrt”, “log2”, None}, int or float, default=1.0\nL690: \nL691: \nL692: The number of features to consider when looking for the best split:\nL693: \nL694:   * If int, then consider `max_features` features at each split.\nL695:   * If float, then `max_features` is a fraction and `max(1, int(max_features * n_features_in_))` features are considered at each split.\nL696: \nL697:   * If “sqrt”, then `max_features=sqrt(n_features)`.\nL698: \nL699:   * If “log2”, then `max_features=log2(n_features)`.\nL700: \nL701:   * If None or 1.0, then `max_features=n_features`.\nL702: \nL703: Note\nL704: \nL705: The default of 1.0 is equivalent to bagged trees and more randomness can be achieved by setting smaller values, e.g. 0.3.\nL706: \nL707: Changed in version 1.1: The default of `max_features` changed from `\"auto\"` to 1.0.\nL708: Note: the search for a split does not stop until at least one valid partition of the node samples is found, even if it requires to effectively inspect more than `max_features` features.\nL709: \nL710: max_leaf_nodes int, default=None\nL711: \nL712: \nL713: Grow trees with `max_leaf_nodes` in best-first fashion. Best nodes are defined as relative reduction in impurity. If None then unlimited number of leaf nodes.\nL714: \nL715: min_impurity_decrease float, default=0.0\n"
  }
````
````text
]
````
